. The source conjugate to
\mathchoice{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
is
{\mathchoice{j^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}\equiv-\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
. The wave operator is
{\mathchoice{\mathcal{O}^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.39969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.39969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.39969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}{\mathcal{O}^{{{{{\mu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.98012pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.39969pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.7081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.83316pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.93166pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.08809pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{{\nu_{\mathcal{B}}}}}}}}\equiv-\alpha\mathchoice{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}\Box-\beta\mathchoice{\partial^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\make up to total derivatives.

III.1
Spin projection

Theoretical development

Projection by spin
J
and parity
P
becomes useful when a preferred frame is available. The particle four-momentum
\mathchoice{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is timelike or null for massive and massless particles, respectively. In the timelike case we may define a unit-timelike vector

\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}/k,\quad k\equiv\sqrt{\mathchoice{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}},

(15)

so that
\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
provides a preferred frame in which it coincides with the observer’s four-velocity

\left[\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\right]^{\mathrm{T}}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0&0\end{bmatrix}.

(16)

Such a frame constitutes a Cauchy slicing in the flat spacetime, and we will introduce a ‘parallel’ and ‘perpendicular’ notation for indices with respect to such slices. A ‘parallel’ index, with the overbar notation, is thus defined as having zero contraction with
\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
. The
general
tensor fields
{\mathchoice{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}}
were introduced in
Eq.

1
. It is possible to decompose these fields into
J^{P}
sectors

(19)

where
{{\overline{\mu}}_{{J^{P}}}}
is a reduced collection of ‘parallel’ indices specific to the
J^{P}
state. The term
reduced
here implies that there may be fewer indices than in
{\mu_{X}}
for some or all of the fields
X
which contain states with this
J^{P}

Eq.

29
. The reduced-index SPOs in
Eq.

19
are constructed from
\mathchoice{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{\eta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}
,

and
\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

(24)

The final equality of
24
leaves the actual
{\mathchoice{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}}{}
components ambiguous, i.e. it does not define the individual elements which match the dimensions of the
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
matrix. From
Eqs.

21
,
22
and
23
the
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
matrix has one
scalar
-valued element (linear in the Lagrangian couplings of
Eq.

1
, and rational in
k
from
Eq.

15
), for each
\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}},{\mathchoice{{j}^{{{Y}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{j}^{{{Y}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{j}^{{{Y}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{j}^{{{Y}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)
pair. However, each
{\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}
state is contributing a
quadratic form
of tensor components to
24
, rather than the square of a scalar. One way to properly accommodate these hidden multiplicities would be to modify
Eq.

22
, so that
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
is built up from the blocks
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}}\otimes{\mathsf{1}}
, where
{\mathsf{1}}
is the
(2J+1)\times(2J+1)
identity, and to separate out the azimuthal spin states in
{\mathchoice{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}}{}
with some polarisation basis vectors. Since this is complicated, we gloss over all such details within this section, and treat each
{\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}
component in
{\mathchoice{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}}{}
as if it were indeed a scalar. In doing so, we will however need to be mindful when going over to the massless limit in
Section

III.3
that, according to
24
, the squares of these components have a more involved structure. By applying the convolution theorem, and remembering that for real fields
{\mathchoice{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.26219pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.41862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{Y}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.26219pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.41862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{Y}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.26219pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.41862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{Y}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.29593pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.26219pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.41862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{Y}}}}}}}(x)
we have
{\mathchoice{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}}(-k)\equiv{\mathchoice{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}}(k)^{*}

\delta S_{\text{F}}=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}k\ \delta{\mathchoice{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot\left[\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}{\upzeta}}{}-{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}\right]\approx 0,

where the total derivatives used in
Eq.

27
to extract the field equations as an overall right-factor under the integral ensure that the
physical
part
\tilde{\mathsf{O}}\left(k\right)
of the wave operator
\mathsf{O}\left(k\right)
— i.e. the part appearing in the field equations — is given by the formula

\tilde{\mathsf{O}}\left(k\right)\equiv-\frac{\left(\mathsf{A}+\mathsf{A}^{\mathrm{T}}\right)k^{2}}{2}-\frac{i\left(\mathsf{B}-\mathsf{B}^{\mathrm{T}}\right)k}{2}+\frac{\left(\mathsf{C}+\mathsf{C}^{\mathrm{T}}\right)}{2}.

(28)

We conclude from
Eq.

28
that the physical part
\tilde{\mathsf{O}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathsf{O}+\mathsf{O}^{\dagger}\right)
is just the
Hermitian
part of the original wave operator. Indeed, using similar total derivatives, the original wave operator in
Eq.

26
can always be made to be Hermitian, so that
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}\equiv{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\equiv\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}}
. This argument also extends to higher-derivative models. Going forwards, we will always assume Hermicity, since it simplifies the computations. The matrices displayed in
ParticleSpectrum
outputs are always Hermitian.

III.2
Massive spectrum

Theoretical development

In
Section

III.1
we reduced the position-space action in
Eq.

1
to the canonical momentum-space matrix form in
Eq.

25
. We now proceed to evaluate the saturated propagator. The wave operator block for a certain
J^{P}
may have a collection of (normalised, but not necessarily orthogonal) null right eigenvectors

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\equiv 0,\quad{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\equiv 1,

(33)

so that
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}
are the null left eigenvectors by the Hermicity of
\mathsf{O}
. Because of the presence of null eigenvectors, or repeated zero eigenvalues,
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
is generically singular. The nullity is equal to the number of independent gauge symmetries (or the number of gauge
generators
) in the theory. Variations
\delta\upzeta^{\mathrm{T}}(-k)\equiv\delta{\mathchoice{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\upzeta^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}=\bigoplus_{J,P}\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{g^{{{*}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g^{{{*}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g^{{{*}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g^{{{*}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}
parameterised by some scalars
{\mathchoice{g_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{g_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\in\mathbb{C}
are purely gauge, and by
Eq.

27
they imply the on-shell source constraints

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{},\quad{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\equiv\bigoplus_{J,P}\left[\mathsf{1}-\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\right].

(34)

When source gauge symmetries or source constraints are present, the naïve formula
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}^{-1}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}
for the saturated propagator fails. This problem can be solved by the addition of gauge-fixing terms to the original formulation in
Eq.

1
. The gauge-fixed wave operator
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}+\bigoplus_{J,P}\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}
is invertible for non-zero gauge-fixing parameters
{\mathchoice{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
, and taking care to use the physical sources
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}
, the saturated propagator is

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\cdot\left[{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}+\bigoplus_{J,P}\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\right]^{-1}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}.

(35)

We do not really care about the advantages of various gauge fixings at tree level, so it is convenient to set all the
{\mathchoice{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{h_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}
to unity in
Eq.

35
and find on-shell

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{},

(36)

where
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}
is the unique Moore–Penrose
[
37
,
38
]
or Drazin
[
81
]
pseudoinverse of the Hermitian wave operator
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\equiv{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}\cdot\left[{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}+\bigoplus_{J,P}\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\right]^{-1}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}{\mathsf{U}}}{}.

(37)

Having obtained the definition of the saturated propagator in
Eq.

36
, we are ready to extract its poles, which correspond to propagating particle states. According to
Eq.

37
, all the poles correspond to the zeroes in
k
of the
J^{P}
determinants

\det\left[{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}}+\sum_{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\right]\propto\prod_{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}\left[k^{2}-{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\right],

(38)

where the proportionality in
Eq.

38
holds up to constants and even powers of
k
, and where
{s_{{J^{P}}}}
label distinct non-vanishing masses (if any), whose no-tachyon conditions are

{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}>0\quad\forall J,P,\quad\forall{s_{{J^{P}}}}.

(39)

Note that a fundamental assumption will be that for the theory in
Eq.

1
we have

{\mathchoice{M_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\neq{\mathchoice{M_{{{{s{{}^{\prime}}}{}_{{{J{{}^{\prime}}}{}^{P{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}}{M_{{{{s{{}^{\prime}}}{}_{{{J{{}^{\prime}}}{}^{P{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}}{M_{{{{s{{}^{\prime}}}{}_{{{J{{}^{\prime}}}{}^{P{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}}{M_{{{{s{{}^{\prime}}}{}_{{{J{{}^{\prime}}}{}^{P{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}}}\quad\forall J,{J{{}^{\prime}}}{},P,{P{{}^{\prime}}}{},\quad\forall{s_{{J^{P}}}},{{s{{}^{\prime}}}{}_{{{J{{}^{\prime}}}{}^{P{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}.

(40)

The possibility of even-power
k
prefactors in
Eq.

38
suggests that there may be null poles associated with
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}
in
Eq.

36
. The question of whether these poles really persist on the lightcone, and whether they correspond to massless states, will need to be more carefully addressed in
Section

III.3
. The massive states are meanwhile easier to analyse. If
Eq.

40
holds, then it can be shown
[
22
]
that the no-ghost condition is

{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{k^{2}\mapsto{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{s_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}}}\left(P\Tr{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{{{J^{P}}}}}}}\right)>0\quad\forall J,P,\quad\forall{s_{{J^{P}}}}.

(41)

Collectively,
Eqs.

39
and
41
are the unitarity conditions on the massive sector of the theory in
Eq.

1
, where the additional assumption of
Eq.

40
has been introduced.

where
\mathchoice{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}

\mathchoice{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}
are respectively the
J^{P}=0^{+}

parts of the field, and similarly for the conjugate source. Let us first assume that the couplings in
3
avoid the following finely-tuned configurations

\alpha+\beta\neq 0,\quad\alpha\neq 0,\quad\beta\neq 0,\quad\gamma\neq 0.

(43)

We will motivate the inequalities in
Eq.

43
by showing that they correspond to special cases of the theory. As a first step we note that they allow the saturated propagator to be defined using
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}^{-1}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}
without any singularities

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{\left(\alpha+\beta\right)k^{2}+\gamma}+\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makeb k^{2}+\gamma}.

(44)

It is then clear from
Eqs.

44
and
43
that there are two distinct massive poles

{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}}\approx-\frac{\gamma}{\alpha+\beta},\quad{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}}\approx-\frac{\gamma}{\alpha}.

(45)

The positivity of the square masses in
Eq.

45
— the
no-tachyon
conditions in
Eq.

39
— are easy to ensure via further inequalities on the couplings. Except for special cases,
Eq.

40
will also be satisfied automatically. However, the positivity of the residues of
Eq.

44
— the
no-ghost
conditions in
Eq.

41
— involve

{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{k^{2}\mapsto{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}}}}\left({\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\right)\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{\left(\alpha+\beta\right)},\quad{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{k^{2}\mapsto{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}}}}\left({\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\right)\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\overline{\mu}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\overline{\mu}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\overline{\mu}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\overline{\mu}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$ (46)

By moving into the rest frame of either particle we can use

to argue that
\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}

. We now consider a special case by relaxing

(48)

Once again, there are no singularities or source constraints involved simply because
\gamma\neq 0
. However this time the whole of the
1^{-}
sector is non-propagating. The
0^{+}
mass in
Eq.

45
survives, and it is easy to see that

47
is made fully self-consistent by the conditions

\alpha=0,\quad\beta>0,\quad\gamma<0.

(49)

This model was considered in
Fig.

10
. As another alternative to the general case, we next relax

43
to

\beta=-\alpha\neq 0,\quad\gamma\neq 0.

(50)

With

the saturated propagator becomes

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{\gamma}+\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle k^{2}+\gamma},

(51)

and by comparing
Eq.

51
with
Eq.

48
we see that the result is complementary to the longitudinal case. The fully consistent version of
Eq.

50
is

\beta=-\alpha>0,\quad\gamma<0.

(52)

This case was Proca theory, as studied already in
Fig.

6
. Finally, it is worth commenting on the case

\alpha\neq 0,\quad\beta=0,\quad\gamma\neq 0.

(53)

Using
Eq.

53
we find from
Eq.

45
that
{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{0^{+}}}}}}}}\approx{\mathchoice{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}{M^{{{2}}}_{{{1_{{1^{-}}}}}}}}
. In this case, two unrelated
J^{P}
sectors share a mass, and the assumption in
Eq.

40
no longer holds. This is not a physically mysterious scenario, and extensions to the algorithm could in principle be developed. For the case in hand, it is clear that the no-ghost condition of the model
Eq.

53
is violated because the residue of the propagator

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{\alpha k^{2}+\gamma},

(54)

is not positive-definite in any frame. So far, we were concerned only with the massive poles in
Eq.

45
. The residues of such poles are very easy to inspect because the propagator retains its covariant
J^{P}
-decomposed form. However, theories can also contain
massless
poles at
k=0
. By comparing with
Eqs.

15
and
16
, we see that the SPO formulation will break down as we approach the null cone.

III.3
Massless spectrum

Theoretical development

In the massless case, it is no longer possible to define
Eqs.

15
and
16
. Prior to taking the
{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{k\mapsto 0}}\big({\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\big)
limit, the SPO decomposition may still be used to obtain
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}
according to
Eq.

36
. In this limit, however, the individual
J^{P}
states
{\mathchoice{j\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}
of the source — defined analogously to the field states
{\mathchoice{\zeta\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}{\zeta\big({\mathchoice{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}{{i}^{{{X}}}_{{{{{J}^{P}}}}}}}\big)^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.80634pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.62679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.78322pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\mu_{X}}}}}}}
in
Eq.

17b
— will tend to disintegrate. This makes it difficult to study the pole residues covariantly, as we did throughout
Section

III.2
. For this reason, it is useful to move to an entirely component-based approach, with a preferred choice of frame

\left[\mathchoice{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\right]^{\mathrm{T}}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{E}&0&0&p\end{bmatrix},\quad k^{2}=\mathcal{E}^{2}-p^{2},

(55)

in which
\mathcal{E}
and
p
are respectively the particle energy and momentum. We use
2p{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathcal{E}\mapsto p}}\big({\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\big)
to study the
{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{k\mapsto 0}}\big({\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\big)
limit. Not all components of the sources should contribute to the saturated propagator. When massive poles are studied covariantly, the pseudoinverse
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}
annihilates the null space of
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
, and so the parts of
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}
proportional to this null space are safely eliminated in
Eq.

36
to leave only the constrained, on-shell sources in
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}
. For massless poles, where tensor components are used instead, only the on-shell components of the sources need to be included: we will calculate these in advance. The null space of
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}{\mathsf{O}}}{}
implies a total of
{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}
independent constraint equation components from a collection of (generally fewer than
{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}
) covariant constraint equations
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{a_{{J^{P}}}}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}\approx 0
. These equations define the shell of the source currents, and we can express them in a matrix-component form

3}_{{X_{\mathrm{L}}}{}}}}}}}}}}{\mathchoice{j^{{{{{{3\dots 3}_{X_{\mathrm{L}}}{}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[20.85378pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[20.85378pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.93678pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[12.4868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j^{{{{{{3\dots 3}_{X_{\mathrm{L}}}{}}}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[20.85378pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[20.85378pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.93678pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[12.4868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{j^{{{{{{3\dots (56)

where
{X_{\mathrm{F}}}{}
and
{X_{\mathrm{L}}}{}
are respectively the first and last fields in the whole series of independent tensor fields, and likewise
{J_{\mathrm{F}}}{}
and
{J_{\mathrm{L}}}{}
and
{P_{\mathrm{F}}}{}
and
{P_{\mathrm{L}}}{}
and
{a_{\mathrm{F}}}{}
and
{a_{\mathrm{L}}}
are the first and last spins, parities and null vector labels which are found to be present in the given model. We can write

as
{\mathsf{V}}{}\cdot{\mathsf{J}}{}\approx 0
where
{\mathsf{V}}{}
is a complex
{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}\times{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}
matrix and the component vector
{\mathsf{J}}{}
is a convenient way to store a total of
{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}
off-shell components of the sources. The on-shell sources can then be reparameterised in terms of a reduced number of variables
\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}\in\mathbb{C}

{\mathsf{J}}{}\approx\sum_{b}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}\big(\mathcal{E}-p\big)^{n_{b}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}},\quad{\mathsf{V}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}}\equiv 0,\quad{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}}\equiv 1,

(57)

where the
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}
are the
{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}-{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}
right null vectors
9
9

9

There is no expectation that
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{{b}}}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.6683pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.46664pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.6683pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.46664pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.6683pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.46664pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.88081pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.6683pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.46664pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b{b{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}
.
of
{\mathsf{V}}{}
, and
{n_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq}
is defined as the minimum power such that

{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathcal{E}\mapsto p}}\left(\big(\mathcal{E}-p\big)^{n_{b}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}{\mathsf{v}_{{{b}}}}}\right)\equiv 0.

(58)

Without
Eq.

it would be possible for spurious massless poles to enter into
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}
through the reparameterisation. We can then identify the
\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}
with the elements of a column vector
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}}{}\in\mathbb{C}^{{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}-{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}}
so that

can be re-expressed as

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{j}^{{{\dagger}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.66667pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17778pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55556pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{O}^{{{+}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}{\mathsf{j}}}{}\approx{\mathchoice{\mathsf{K}^{{{\dagger}}}}{\mathsf{K}^{{{\dagger}}}}{\mathsf{K}^{{{\dagger}}}}{\mathsf{K}^{{{\dagger}}}}}{}\cdot{\mathsf{\Pi}}{}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}{\maths (59)

for some Hermitian
({\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}-{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{})\times({\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}-{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{})
matrix
{\mathsf{\Pi}}{}
which depends on the momentum components
\mathcal{E}
and
p
and the coupling coefficients in
Eq.

1
. In turn, there may be some
non
-null eigenvectors
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}}\in\mathbb{C}^{{\mathsf{j}_{\text{T}}}{}-{\mathsf{v}_{\text{T}}}{}}
with eigenvalues
{\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
such that

{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathcal{E}\mapsto p}}\left({\mathsf{\Pi}}{}\right)\cdot{\mathchoice{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}}\equiv{\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}}{\mathchoice{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}{\mathsf{u}_{{{c}}}}}.

(60)

Each such eigenvector signals the presence of a propagating massless d.o.f. The situation where
{\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}}={\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{{c{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\lambda_{{{{c{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\lambda_{{{{c{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}{\lambda_{{{{c{{}^{\prime}}}{}}}}}}
for one or more pairs
c
and
{c{{}^{\prime}}}{}
indicates that a single massless particle has multiple polarisations. The total no-ghost condition for the massless sector is

{\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}}>0,\quad\forall c.

(61)

It should be noted from
Eqs.

57
,
59
and
60
that the
{\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}{\lambda_{{{c}}}}}
in general are rational functions not only in the coupling coefficients, but also of the square of the null momentum component
p^{2}
>0. This extra variable can sometimes obfuscate the no-ghost conditions in
Eq.

61
, and in such cases it may be helpful to seek further momentum-dependent reparameterisations of
{\mathchoice{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}{\mathsf{K}}}{}
which simplify the eigenvalues.

Examples

It can be shown that there are no massless poles for the theories considered in
Eqs.

43
,
47
,
50
and
53
. We will therefore consider extra critical cases of the theory in which such poles are present. We first assume

\alpha+\beta\neq 0,\quad\alpha\neq 0,\quad\gamma=0,

(62)

so that according to the principles above, the saturated propagator implied by
42
is

{\mathchoice{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}{\mathsf{P}}}{}\approx\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\perp}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{\perp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}{\left(\alpha+\beta\right)k^{2}}+\frac{\mathchoice{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00002pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\overline{\mu}}}}}{{J^{\dagger}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.00 k^{2}}.

(63)

It follows from
Eq.

that there are no gauge symmetries in the model, hence no source constraints. In this case

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathchoice{\mathcal{J}^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{J}^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\\

(67)

The second term in

can be made to be a positive-definite quadratic form if

, so
{\ \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathcal{E}\mapsto p}}\left({\mathsf{\Pi}}{}\right)

be said of the first term in

so long as the conditions in
